**Prior update:** 2026-08-24 (`CG-S15-HDN-011` — **Performance and Scalability
(Prompt 379)** — `VERIFIED`, Tier C closed. Four independent parallel adversarial
lenses ran against the first-round commit (`57ce9fb`). **Lens 4 (attack-surface)
found and this checkpoint fixed a real structural weakening in its own headline
fix**: the first-draft `edge` CTE rewrite dropped a `\m` word-boundary anchor and a
real join-against-`fn` requirement the original self-join carried "for free" —
live-forced 876 spurious edges on the real 2,700-function schema (zero colliding
with any real function name, so no wrong verdict today, but a future collision
could have silently defeated the guard); fixed by adding
`and m[1] in (select proname from fn)` (a hash semi-join, not a new cross join)
and restoring the `\m` anchor, re-timed at **1.66 seconds**, both gaps confirmed
closed via live reproduction with 3 scratch functions. **Lens 1 (correctness
re-derivation) found the first round's own timing precision did not reproduce**:
an independent same-schema matched-pair re-measurement got **212,105.6ms/≈313×**
rather than **692,092.8ms/1244×** — both real, honest measurements; the ~3% spread
reflects real sandbox contention variance at measurement time, cite
"300×-1200×+" going forward, not a fixed multiplier. Also found `ISS-2026-239`'s
claim that no RPC filters through `audit_logs.actor_auth_user_id` was factually
wrong (`app.search_audit_logs` does, zero live UI callers today — the "don't
index yet" conclusion is unchanged, the evidence was fixed). **Lens 2
(schema-wide completeness sweep) found 5 new unbounded-dataset instances**
`ISS-2026-238` missed, most notably a 4-list unbounded fleet-assets page
(`listVehicleOperationalProfiles`/`listDriverOperationalProfiles`/
`listGpsDevices`/`listSimCards`); everything else (more O(n²) self-joins, an
independent unindexed-FK sample, other RLS-performance patterns,
cache/backpressure) came back clean. **Lens 3 (ledger/documentation consistency)
found and this checkpoint corrected 6 real documentation miscounts**: `auth.*()`
call-site count (236→235), `default auth.uid()` pattern count (73/~40→72/35), a
stale "~2,900 functions" figure left beside its own "2,700" correction, 2
never-updated `BLOCKER_LEDGER.md` entries, a misleading FK-table-count phrasing,
and a minor line-citation drift. `ISS-2026-238` corrected and expanded:
`listFilesForTenant` reclassified Medium (a polymorphic, transactional-volume

of replacing it — Postgres does not treat an appended trailing parameter as a
signature match for `CREATE OR REPLACE` — which would have left every existing

function's own origin migration to restore the exact original `authenticated,

Eight migrations, nine independent findings, all live-forced before being fixed rather than fixed on the sweep's say-so. **Headline finding**: `app.evaluate_permission` (the root RBAC gate, ~1,124 callers) never checked tenant membership at all — a revoked ex-member retained every role-based permission indefinitely; live-confirmed end to end (a genuinely revoked user, acting as themselves, successfully called both a read and a write RPC gated by `evaluate_permission`, which returned `allowed=true, reason='role_grant'`). **Fixed** at `supabase/migrations/20260810300000_harden_rbac_evaluator_tenant_membership_check.sql` (`ISS-2026-180`). **Largest finding of Step 15 to date**: the entire Finance manual/period/config/import-export write surface — 95 functions (76 top-level entry points plus 19 `check_finance_*_authority` helpers) plus `app.enqueue_job` — was `SECURITY INVOKER` instead of `SECURITY DEFINER`, and has been completely unreachable by any real `authenticated` session since it shipped. Live-forced: a genuine, non-superuser Finance Manager session calling `app.create_finance_journal_draft` for their own tenant was refused `permission denied for table permissions`, three call frames inside `evaluate_permission` itself. **Fixed** at `supabase/migrations/20260810700000_harden_finance_authority_chain_security_definer.sql` (`HDN-BLK-015`/`ISS-2026-182`), together with two findings its own fix-and-verify cycle surfaced in the same migration: `ISS-2026-183` (`create_and_post_finance_system_journal` had no authority check of its own — reachable for the first time once the grant chain was restored) and `ISS-2026-184` (`finance_journals`/`finance_journal_lines` RLS bypassed `FIN:View` entirely, fixed at `supabase/migrations/20260810800000_harden_finance_journal_view_gate_and_self_approval.sql`, which also carries `ISS-2026-181`'s Finance journal self-approval guard, sharing `ISS-2026-139`'s shape). **Carried forward from `HDN-372` and closed**: `HDN-BLK-012`/`ISS-2026-165` — 13 dashboard functions sharing `HDN-BLK-011`'s actor-forgery shape, fixed at `supabase/migrations/20260810200000_harden_dashboard_actor_identity_gaps.sql` (5 of the 13 additionally gate a field-masking entitlement on the same forged parameter — a genuine unmasking bypass, not merely a record-scope widening). `HDN-BLK-014`/`ISS-2026-179` — of ~30 candidates carried forward, 16 confirmed terminal/self-referential and fixed at `supabase/migrations/20260810400000_harden_crm_ops_actor_identity_gaps.sql`; ~14 confirmed genuinely called with third-party actor arguments elsewhere in the schema and **not** fixed — re-registered narrower as `ISS-2026-186`, needing a per-call-site audit rather than a blind repository-wide edit. `ISS-2026-171`/`173` — own-row RLS gaps on `notifications`/`saved_report_views` post-revocation, fixed at `supabase/migrations/20260810500000_harden_own_row_rls_membership_gap.sql`, together with one further instance found in the same migration (`ISS-2026-185`, `notification_preferences`, identical shape). `ISS-2026-139` (loyalty redemption maker/checker collapse, seeded from `HARDENING_MATRIX.md` §4) — live-forced exactly as described (a "Redemption Clerk" holding only `LYL:Edit` walked two unrelated accounts' real points into real vouchers in one session, zero second reviewer) and **fixed** at `supabase/migrations/20260810600000_harden_loyalty_redemption_maker_checker.sql`. `ISS-2026-137` (Supreme-Admin-override gap) independently re-verified — the table is actually MORE locked down than its siblings (missing both the append-only trigger and any `UPDATE`/`DELETE` grant at all); existing disposition (`OPEN`, Low, "no live vulnerability, only a missing correction path") confirmed accurate, no change made. **Six further findings registered, not fixed, each with a named forward owner**: `ISS-2026-186` (~14 residual shared RBAC primitives, owner a future checkpoint); **Prior update:** 2026-08-18 (`CG-S13-CPL-022..025` — **Reward Catalogue, Redemption Approval and Fulfillment, Expiry and Fraud Prevention, Liability Reconciliation Analytics (Prompts 320-323) — Batch 5** — all `VERIFIED`. **Batch 5 (`CPL-022..025`) is fully closed. All of `CPL-002..025` (Phase 8's kickoff plus Batches 1-5) is now `VERIFIED`.** Each of the four prompts was implemented as its own small, single-agent `Workflow` run, continuing Batch 4's own container-restart-mitigation strategy, with only the batch's Tier C review and fix pass combining all four diffs. CPL-320 (Reward Catalogue): the first reward-definition schema — effective-dated draft/published/paused/archived lifecycle mirroring CPL-317's tier-versioning shape, plus a race-safe stock-reservation primitive (`app.reserve_loyalty_reward_stock_unit`) deliberately left uncalled for CPL-321 to compose (`ISS-2026-131`). CPL-321 (Redemption Approval and Fulfillment): composes CPL-320's stock reservation, CPL-318's points-consumption, and CPL-319's entitlement-issuance at the moment of redemption; resolves a genuine dual-authority design tension via `app.submit_loyalty_redemption` always creating a real `pending_approval` intent record first, then attempting inline composition that only succeeds synchronously for a caller independently holding `LYL:Edit`; builds genuinely new `physical_item`/`service_credit` fulfillment tracking (no existing primitive to compose). CPL-322 (Expiry and Fraud Prevention): a real, storm-controlled `app.jobs`-backed expiry-sweep RPC mirroring HRT-291's own batch-run shape, composing CPL-318/319's own expire functions; a governed fraud review-case workflow composing CPL-317's own hold/release RPCs (never a second, competing hold table); a suppression/cooldown mechanism mirroring HRT-291's own suppression shape exactly. CPL-323 (Liability Reconciliation Analytics): mirrors FIN-209's execute/resolve-then-certify reconciliation-gate shape as new Loyalty-owned objects; resolves a genuine no-fabricated-conversion-rate design question by reporting points liability as a raw, dimensionless figure, never blended with the four currency-denominated lines; every total recomputed live from raw ledger/event tables each run, formally closing the "Loyalty ledger exactness" evidence citation named at Phase 8 kickoff. **Batch 5's own Tier C review** (4 lenses, each live-tested against a fresh disposable database, seeded with 5 specific items the orchestrating session itself pre-flagged during its own solo-prompt spot-verification) found and fixed at root cause: a **Critical** finding, confirmed independently by all four lenses and matching the orchestrating session's own pre-review hypothesis exactly — `app.submit_loyalty_redemption`'s inline auto-approve composition attempt caught only `insufficient_authority`-prefixed exceptions and re-raised every other failure (a routine, guaranteed-to-occur "reward out of stock" condition, not only a misconfigured reward), aborting the whole transaction and silently destroying the just-inserted `pending_approval` redemption row and its event — directly contradicting the migration's own documented "every submission always creates the intent record" invariant — fixed by widening the catch to swallow every composition failure unconditionally, always preserving the row for a human to resolve via `app.decide_loyalty_redemption`. A second **Critical** finding, found only by the cross-prompt-integration lens — CPL-323's own `reward_fulfillment_liability_total` (the 5th, disclosed liability line) trusted `app.loyalty_redemptions.status` directly with no re-derivation from the append-only event log, unlike the other four liability lines in the same run — a direct, RPC-bypassing status corruption could silently understate certified liability with zero detection — fixed by re-deriving redemption status from its latest event type and adding a new `redemption_liability_status_mismatch` exception type. A **High** finding — CPL-323's own new db-test fixture UUID range genuinely collided with two pre-existing, unrelated files (`finance-cash-bank.sql`, `procurement-sourcing.sql`), breaking the full, unmodified `bash scripts/db-tests/run.sh` harness partway through, its own "grep-verified unclaimed" header claim false — fixed by renumbering to the batch's own next free `346xxx` range. A **Medium** finding — CPL-323's `app.get_customer_portal_loyalty_summary` claimed to compose CPL-319's own list function but instead ran an independent, duplicate raw aggregation (the "second, independently-evolving enforcement point" class `ADR-0024` Part A warns against) — fixed to genuinely call the composed function. A **Low/Medium** defense-in-depth finding — 5 of CPL-320's own version-checked reward-lifecycle functions relied solely on the terminal `UPDATE`'s own repeated version predicate for NULL-bypass safety, missing the explicit up-front rejection every other Batch 5 sibling already carries (confirmed not live-exploitable today, but `app.publish_loyalty_reward` performs a real side-effect write before the late rejection) — fixed by adding the identical explicit prefix to all 5 functions. Two items the orchestrating session flagged were confirmed correct with no fix needed: the CPL-322 fraud hold genuinely, live-provenly blocks a new CPL-321 redemption attempt (a real cross-prompt regression proof, not merely each prompt's own isolated fixture); CPL-320/322's own dual `'loyalty_expiration'`/`'loyalty_expiry_sweep'` `app.jobs.job_type` CHECK-constraint literals are adequately disclosed and the dead placeholder is confirmed never dispatched by any live code path anywhere in the repository. One further pre-existing, unrelated defect was discovered while re-running the full harness after the UUID-collision fix: `scripts/db-tests/hris-shift-roster-scheduling.sql` (100% Phase 7/HRIS scope, zero Batch 5 file reference, untouched by this session per `git log`) belongs to the already-registered day-of-week/wall-clock-dependent fixture-flakiness class (`ISS-2026-077`/`103`/`115`) — registered as `ISS-2026-135`, not fixed, out of this review's own Batch-5/Loyalty charter. **Independent verification performed personally by the orchestrating session** (not accepted from the fix agent's own report): read the widened `submit_loyalty_redemption` exception-catch clause directly, confirming it is now an unconditional `when others then null;`; read `execute_loyalty_liability_reconciliation_run`'s redemption-status loop directly, confirming the liability total now uses the event-log-derived status, never the cached column; confirmed the db-test's real UUID literals were fully migrated off the old colliding range; confirmed all 5 of CPL-320's version-checked functions now carry the explicit NULL-rejection prefix; confirmed `app.get_customer_portal_loyalty_summary` now genuinely composes the customer-facing function. Then independently re-ran every gate fresh: `typecheck` 0 errors; `lint` 0 errors/307 warnings; `pnpm run test` **4823/4823 pass**; the full, unmodified `bash scripts/db-tests/run.sh` (191+ files) ran clean through every Loyalty-domain file (CPL-315..323 all `ALL PASSED`) and both of the fix's own former collision points, halting only at the pre-existing, unrelated `ISS-2026-135` HRIS flake — the ONLY error anywhere in the entire run log, directly confirmed via a full-log grep for `ERROR`; `docs:check`/`security:check`/`git:check-paths` all clean. **`CG-S13-CPL-026` (Prompt 324, Customer Portal and Loyalty Integrated Verification) is dependency-clean and next-eligible — but NOT started this checkpoint**, per the orchestrating session's own explicit operator instruction to stop at Prompt 323/Batch 5's own close (still within the standing "prompt 299-327" operator authorization; no fresh authorization is required to begin it whenever work resumes). **Prior update:** 2026-08-17 (`CG-S13-CPL-017..021` — **Customer User Management, Loyalty Program and Earning, Membership Tier, Points Ledger, Cashback Discount Voucher (Prompts 315-319) — Batch 4** — all `VERIFIED`. **Batch 4 (`CPL-017..021`) is fully closed.** Each of the five prompts was implemented as its own small, single-agent `Workflow` run — a deliberate change from Batches 1-3's larger combined runs, adopted to bound blast radius after two host-environment container restarts occurred earlier in this session — with only the batch's Tier C review and fix pass combining all five diffs, per `AGENTS.md`'s own batched-review requirement. CPL-315 (Customer User Management): closes CPL-300's own two disclosed gaps — a role-update RPC with a race-safe last-active-account_admin guard (locks the full active-admin set `FOR UPDATE` before counting), and a new, separate, append-only access-review table, never an `ALTER TABLE` on CPL-300's applied table; ships the first real invite/role-change/suspend/reactivate/revoke/access-review UI. CPL-316 (Loyalty Program and Earning): the FIRST-EVER Loyalty domain schema in this repository (ADR-0024 Part D) — 4 tables, plus an idempotent `app.evaluate_customer_loyalty_earning_for_paid_invoice` reading (never writing) Finance's already-committed `app.finance_ar_open_items`; the earning-events table mirrors `app.inventory_movements`'s own append-only/reversal shape exactly. CPL-317 (Membership Tier): 3 tables, 11 RPCs — a real downgrade-timing policy (upgrades immediate, downgrades gated on a stamped `next_review_at`) and a fraud-hold mechanism kept on its own orthogonal table, never an inline column on the append-only movements table. CPL-318 (Points Ledger, explicitly the batch's highest financial-integrity-risk prompt): 4 tables incl. a real `generated always as (...) stored` balance column, 17 RPCs around one generic posting primitive, a PRC-264-mirrored maker-checker adjustment pair; self-found and fixed during design (before this checkpoint's own migration was ever run) a ledger-insert-ordering race that would have let a losing racer on a duplicate idempotency key double-count balance. CPL-319 (Cashback Discount Voucher): 2 tables, ~10 RPCs; voucher codes use the repository's established hashed-bearer-secret pattern adapted to a short, human-typeable code via a self-found-and-fixed from-scratch base32 encoder (Postgres core's own `encode()` has no `base32` target); a FIN-209-shaped Finance reconciliation mechanism was explicitly, orchestrator-directedly deferred to `CPL-323`'s own future scope, disclosed as `ISS-2026-129` item 1. **Batch 4's own Tier C review** (4 lenses, each live-tested against a fresh disposable database) found and fixed at root cause: a **Critical** finding, escalated per this build's own standing rule that any balance-manipulation defect is Critical — `app.consume_loyalty_points_fifo` (CPL-318) only iterated active lots, so a positive lot-less manual point adjustment created permanently unspendable balance — fixed by attempting one final lot-less consuming entry once lot iteration exhausts, letting the posting primitive's own authoritative negative-balance guard decide; independently re-verified by the orchestrating session reading the fixed function body directly, confirming the all-or-nothing rollback semantics hold. A **High** finding — CPL-316 (written and merged between CPL-315 and CPL-317) never applied the `now()`→`clock_timestamp()` fix CPL-315 had just self-found and fixed earlier in this same batch, despite CPL-317/318/319 each proactively citing and adopting it — fixed across all 8 affected sites. A **High**-class NULL-bypass optimistic-concurrency defect across 3 functions spanning CPL-315/316/317 (`record_version <> p_expected_version` silently evaluates falsy-NULL when the expected version itself is NULL, bypassing the staleness check if the `UPDATE` does not repeat the predicate) — fixed by repeating `and record_version = p_expected_version` on the `UPDATE` itself in every affected function. A **Medium** documentation-accuracy finding — CPL-318's own design-decision comment misquoted `06_RLS_RBAC_WORKSTREAM.md`'s residual-risk language (§8) while omitting its separate release-blocker language (§13) for Supreme-Admin overrides — fixed via citation correction plus a matching CPL-319 disclosure, and a new `ISS-2026-130` (Medium, `OPEN`) registered: no Supreme-Admin-override mechanism exists yet for any of the 4 new Loyalty ledger tables, a standing gap shared with the pre-existing, already-`VERIFIED` `app.inventory_movements`, not unique to this batch, named as a future dedicated cross-cutting task. Also fixed: an undisclosed nested-authority coupling on 2 functions, formalized with an explicit `LYL:Edit` check. **The orchestrating session additionally found and fixed, independently**, a pre-existing (Batch-3-introduced, confirmed via `git log`/`git diff` before fixing, not Batch-4-caused) test-suppression-metadata gap in `server/mutations/customer-portal-profile.test.ts` (a `@ts-expect-error` comment missing its required `SUPPRESS(...)` metadata line per `CODING_STANDARDS.md` §12), bringing `pnpm run test` from 4682/4683 to 4683/4683. **Independent verification performed personally by the orchestrating session** (not accepted from the fix agent's own report): read the CRITICAL fix and the `clock_timestamp()`/NULL-bypass fixes directly in the migration files, then independently re-ran every gate fresh: `typecheck` 0 errors; `lint` 0 errors/301 warnings; `pnpm run test` **4683/4683 pass**; the full, unmodified `bash scripts/db-tests/run.sh` (189 files) ran clean end to end with zero halt (`==> db-tests: ALL PASSED`, exit 0), directly confirmed via log inspection including all five Batch 4 pass markers (`customer-user-management.sql: ALL PASSED`; `ALL PASSED: CPL-316 Loyalty Program and Earning`; `ALL PASSED: CPL-317 Membership Tier`; `ALL PASSED: CPL-318 Points Ledger`; `ALL PASSED: CPL-319 Cashback Discount Voucher`); `docs:check`/`security:check`/`git:check-paths` all clean. **`CG-S13-CPL-022` (Prompt 320, Reward Catalogue) is dependency-clean and next-eligible**, first prompt of Batch 5, within this session's own "prompt 299-327" operator authorization — requires no fresh authorization. one shared per-type adapter (`app._ticket_link_resolve_candidate`) composes each domain's OWN existing access predicate verbatim — a link never grants access, every read independently re-authorizes; a new `app._ticket_link_actor_may_view_tenant_data` gate closes a genuine C-08 widening a Supreme Admin would otherwise gain via a helpdesk ticket. 4 self-found defects fixed before commit, the most severe a genuine C-05 ordering bug (an existing-link short-circuit ran BEFORE re-validating the current caller's own eligibility) plus two self-found repository-wide `rbac-enforcement.sql` sweep failures on `record_ticket_link_access_denial` (no tenant-authority/claimed-actor check). **Prompt 293 (Sensitive Personal and Payroll Data Controls, `CG-S12-HRT-021`)** was a corrective/hardening checkpoint (no new capability) over all 19 already-VERIFIED HR (274-285) and Ticket (286-292) Phase 7 capabilities: Finding A (CRITICAL) — `app.employees`' 5 HR-narrative lifecycle-reason columns were returned unconditionally by `get_employee_profile` (never masked, unlike national_id/DOB two lines above) plus exposed via unrestricted raw-table grants on `app.employees`/`app.employee_lifecycle_events`, fixed plus a self-found sibling sweep across 5 further tables; Finding B (CRITICAL) — the previously-claimed-closed C-24 unmasked-audit-log-reason leak was live across most of Phase 7, 37 `capture_audit_event` call sites fixed across Employee Master/Leave/Recruitment-ATS/Onboarding/Attendance/Shift-Roster, including a distinct raw-score vector in `record_assessment_result`; 2 HIGH fixed (5 new data-classification registry entries; the FIN-only adoption gate extended with an HRS mirror, plus a self-found `PAYROLL_REGISTRY`-never-wired-into-`REGISTRY` gap); 3 Medium disclosed not fixed (`ISS-2026-091`/`092`/`093`), each genuinely outside this checkpoint's bounded scope. New `docs/standards/HR_PAYROLL_FIELD_POLICY_MATRIX.md` published, mirroring `FINANCE_FIELD_POLICY_MATRIX.md`'s structure. Gate suite fresh after all three Implement stages: `typecheck` 0 errors; `lint` 0/271 (unchanged baseline); `pnpm run test` 4112/4112; `db:test` ALL PASSED with one independently-proven-pre-existing flake set aside (`ISS-2026-077`); `next build` clean (5 new routes across the batch). **The batch's own combined 4-lens Tier C review (against the merged `291-293` diff, one review not three, per `BUILD_EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.md` §5) found 9 CONFIRMED findings, 0 rejected — 1 CRITICAL, 1 CRITICAL-partial re-assessment, 2 HIGH (one a re-assessment), 2 MEDIUM, 3 disclosed-not-fixed (2 Medium, 1 Low).** Most severe (**CRITICAL**, security lens, `ISS-2026-094`): `app.ticket_links`/`app.ticket_link_events` raw-table SELECT granted exactly the cross-domain, cross-tenant access `app.list_ticket_links`'s own RPC layer exists to deny — live-reproduced twice (a same-tenant zero-permission staff member raw-reading masked invoice/vendor/shipment content; a cross-tenant, zero-grant Supreme Admin raw-reading a helpdesk ticket's linked vendor record) — fixed by revoking raw SELECT from `authenticated` on both tables entirely. **HIGH** (integration lens, `ISS-2026-095`): 5 pre-existing HR/Recruitment RPCs silently bypassed Prompt 293's own raw-table column-restriction sweep via `select *`/a full composite, live-reproduced with real disciplinary/PIP/background-check narrative reaching a plain `HRS:View` holder — all 5 re-defined with the established masking guard. **2 MEDIUM** (correctness/spec-compliance, `ISS-2026-096`/`097`): `app.escalate_ticket`'s double-submit protection was illusory whenever the escalation doesn't itself mutate `app.tickets` (live-raced, two concurrent identical calls both succeeded) — fixed with a dedicated advisory-lock-serialized guard; `app.adjust_leave_balance`/`app.load_opening_leave_balance` had a real unique index and pre-check but no exception-handler third leg of the C-01/C-02 idempotency shape — fixed. **CRITICAL, partial re-assessment** (`ISS-2026-098`, closes the raw-table half of already-disclosed `ISS-2026-093`): `app.cancel_approval_request` also writes the raw reason into `app.approval_requests.ended_reason`, readable via a pre-existing blanket grant to any active tenant member, a lower bar than the already-disclosed audit-log route — the raw-table vector fixed here (column-restricted grant), the audit-log-duplication half remains genuinely `OPEN` (Platform Core PLT-123, correctly outside this batch's HR-scoped mandate). **HIGH re-assessment** (`ISS-2026-099`, closes `ISS-2026-092`): `app.employee_change_requests.reason`/`decided_reason` were readable by any active tenant member via the identical column-restricted-grant-still-including-the-column shape as Finding A — closed with a new masked `app.get_employee_change_requests` RPC plus matching server/UI wiring. 3 findings disclosed, not fixed, each genuinely outside this review's additive-migration-plus-narrow-TS-edit mandate: `ISS-2026-100` (`app.list_ticket_link_events` has no UI caller), `ISS-2026-101` (customer-visible escalation status has no reachable UI caller for the actual `customer_user` principal, mirrors an already-accepted HRT-289 precedent), `ISS-2026-102` (a customer viewer of `app.list_ticket_links` sees raw `entity_type`/`entity_id` for an out-of-registry link type, Low). Two further day-of-week-dependent pre-existing `db:test` flakes were found alongside the already-registered `ISS-2026-077` (`hris-overtime-timesheet.sql`, `procurement-vendor-performance.sql`, both outside this batch's changed files, neither touched, `ISS-2026-103`). **Prior update:** 2026-08-09 (`CG-S12-HRT-005` — **Onboarding and Offboarding (Prompt 277)** — `VERIFIED`. **Second task of the "lanjut 276-280" operator authorization**, run as its own single-prompt Tier-C batch (§3.2 trigger 3: writes through Platform identity/access authority — the first Phase 7 capability to exercise the governed candidate→employee→user linkage ADR-0023 Part B reserved for it). `PHASE_7_IN_PROGRESS` remains in progress (5/24 Phase 7 capability rows now `VERIFIED`). The candidate→employee→user conversion correctly reuses existing Platform primitives throughout (`app.create_employee_draft`, `app.invite_user`/`app.link_employee_user`/`app.assign_role`, `app.transition_user_status`), never a second identity-writing path. 11 self-found defects fixed before commit, the major one a taxonomy-C-01 idempotency gap found by the mandatory Tier B walk itself, not a db-test failure. Gate suite fresh after Implement: `typecheck` 0 errors; `lint` 0/205; `pnpm run test` 3578/3578; `db:test` ALL PASSED; `next build` clean (5 new routes). **Tier C review (4 parallel adversarial lenses, live-tested) found the largest, most severe finding set of any Phase 7 checkpoint so far: 12 CONFIRMED, 4 CRITICAL, 3 HIGH, 4 MEDIUM, 1 LOW, 0 rejected.** Most severe: a **live-exploited privilege-escalation path** — `app.request_onboarding_access_provisioning` wrapped the previously service_role-only `app.assign_role` behind mere `HRS:Edit`, letting any ordinary HR/onboarding staffer grant ANY published tenant role — including cross-domain Finance/Procurement/Operations Approve/Override/Delete permissions — to themselves or anyone else; live-reproduced twice (third-party grant and self-grant) with `evaluate_permission` flipping to `true` on protected cross-domain permissions for an attacker holding only ordinary `HRS:Edit`. A second CRITICAL: `cancel_onboarding_case` never revoked Platform access already granted by a concurrent/completed provisioning task, live-reproduced with two real concurrent `psql` sessions. A third CRITICAL: `start_onboarding_case`'s idempotency-replay tuple, though newly added this same checkpoint, was still incomplete for the `direct_hire` path. A fourth CRITICAL: access revocation was materially incomplete — `app.transition_user_status`'s revoke branch never touched `app.role_assignments`, so a "revoked" actor live-reproducibly retained full write authority (successfully created a new onboarding case after being revoked). **All 4 CRITICAL plus 5 further findings fixed** via a new additive migration (`20260730890000`): the provisioning grant path now requires `HRS:Override` plus a new delegation check (`app.assert_actor_holds_role_version_permissions` — an actor may never grant a permission they don't themselves hold); cancel now revokes the case's own completed access grants; the idempotency tuple widened to the full payload; revocation now explicitly revokes every active `role_assignments` row for the target. 2 findings disclosed as new-scope known issues: `ISS-2026-072` (OPEN, High — the systemic PLT-110/112 gap beneath the revocation fix: `evaluate_permission` never checks user/tenant-identity status at all, a broader primitive-level gap than this one call site can fully close) and `ISS-2026-073` (OPEN, Medium — no "approved direct hire" precondition despite the prompt naming one). **Gate suite independently re-run a third time by the orchestrating session, every number matching**: `typecheck` 0 errors; `lint` 0/205; `pnpm run test` **3579/3579**; `pnpm run db:test` **ALL PASSED** (190 migrations); `next build` clean, 5 routes present; `git:check-paths`/`security:check`/`docs:check`/`standards:check`/`data-classification:check`/`threat-model:check`/`security:audit` all clean. Direct code reads independently confirmed the `HRS:Override` gate, the delegation check, the cancel-revokes-grants logic, and the explicit `role_assignments` revocation loop are each genuinely present and correctly shaped. **Prior checkpoint note:** 2026-08-07 (**out-of-band governance change — batched review-and-fix execution cadence ratified as `ADR-0021`.** `COMPLETED`. Not a prompt-package task, and **no capability prompt was executed**: this checkpoint sits between the already-`VERIFIED` Prompt 256 (`CG-S11-PRC-007`) and the still-unauthorized Prompt 257 (`CG-S11-PRC-008`), and it does not itself authorize Prompt 257 or any further Phase 6 work. Operator instruction: revise the AI agent execution rules so review and fix run **every 5 prompts rather than every prompt**, while still preserving clean code, inter-data dependency correctness, security, absence of errors and potential errors, and freedom from regression — motivated by a hard project deadline of **2026-08-24** against **174 remaining prompts (257–430)**, at a measured cost of roughly two hours of wall-clock per prompt under the prior cadence. **The prior cadence was never a written rule.** Prompts 220–256 ran "implement → 3-lens adversarial review → fix → independent re-verification" after every single prompt as a practice recorded only retrospectively in build logs and `docs/build-log/phase-06/PROCUREMENT_VENDOR_EXECUTION_INDEX.md` — which is exactly why changing it required a real decision record rather than a silent practice change. **What the evidence showed before the design was chosen:** the round's yield is real and concentrated (Prompts 251–256 alone produced ~40 independently-verified defects, several Critical/High that would otherwise have shipped — an unlocked single-use invitation token creating two vendor profiles, a `jsonb` snapshot leaking `budget_amount` around its own typed sibling's `PRC:View cost` mask and real customer PII to the browser's RSC payload, an import handler catching every `unique_violation` and silently dropping a rate while reporting success), so removing the round was never an option; but read across `docs/build-log/phase-05/ATW-030.md`/`ATW-031.md`/`ATW-032.md` and `docs/build-log/phase-06/PRC-251.md`–`PRC-256.md`, **more than 90 defects collapse into 20 recurring classes**, and every Phase 6 Critical/High falls into one of them. **Decisively: the classes recurred even with per-prompt review in place** — Prompt 256 reintroduced the exact session-implicit-`auth.uid()` defect Prompt 255 had already fixed; Prompts 252/253/254 each independently reintroduced the idempotency-comparison class `CG-S10-ATW-030` had already swept across 20 functions. Per-prompt review was catching recurrence after the fact, not preventing it. **Design adopted:** the single round splits into three tiers — **Tier A** automated gates (`typecheck`/`lint`/`db:test`/`test`/`git:check-paths`/`security:check`, plus `next build` whenever `app/`, `components/`, or a `"use server"` module is touched) **per prompt, blocking**; **Tier B** a mandatory walk of the new `docs/standards/RECURRING_DEFECT_TAXONOMY.md` §4 against the agent's own diff, recorded in the prompt's build log, **per prompt, blocking** — this is the compensating control that makes batching safe, and it attacks recurrence at write time, which per-prompt review never did; **Tier C** the adversarial review + fix pass + propagation sweep + independent full-suite re-verification, **once per batch of at most five prompts**. Tier C gains a **fourth review lens — cross-prompt integration and data dependency** — added specifically for the risk batching magnifies: this build's capabilities are demonstrably written by mirroring the previous capability's shape (Prompt 252 mirrored `app.item_control_policy_versions`, 253 mirrored 252, 254 mirrored "252/253's twice-proven shape", 256 mirrored `app.vendor_profile_lifecycle_events`), so an uncaught defect is *copied* forward. Six triggers cut a batch short immediately (any Critical/High finding; a first-of-its-kind security mechanism; any prompt touching finance posting/the RBAC evaluator/RLS primitives/auth; a destructive migration; a Tier A failure not clearable in-prompt; a dependency edge that would separate a consumer from its producer), and batch size is **adaptive** — 5 after a clean batch, ≤4 after one Critical/High, ≤3 after two or more until a batch closes clean. Verification, hardening, release and final-validation prompts (phase closure sets, 368–389, 390–412, 413–430) are **never** batched. `COMPLETED` now explicitly means "adversarially unreviewed"; only a batch's Tier C close moves prompts to `VERIFIED`, and a batch is all-or-nothing. **Nothing was weakened:** no gate removed, relabelled, or made optional (the full suite still runs fresh before any prompt is `VERIFIED`); applied migrations still may never be edited, so a late-found defect is repaired by a corrective migration exactly as `20260730380000_harden_advanced_tms_wms_idempotency_target_mismatch.sql`, `20260730480000_harden_optimistic_concurrency_row_lock.sql` and `20260730520000_harden_stale_version_no_op_and_swallowed_idempotency_guard.sql` already precedent; no Critical/High finding may be left open uncontained; independent re-verification by the orchestrating session remains mandatory and may not be replaced by a fix agent's self-report; findings are still re-derived before they are fixed (`CG-S10-ATW-032`'s 20-confirmed/7-already-fixed/5-by-design disposition of 32 inherited claims is the standing reason). **Stated honestly rather than implied:** batching does not shorten the Step 15 hardening or Step 16 release/go-live sequences, which are what establish publish-readiness; and it does not on its own make 174 prompts fit before 2026-08-24 — parallel implementation across independent dependency-graph branches is the other required lever, and any claim the deadline is met must rest on evidence at the time. `ADR-0021` records an explicit reversal condition: two consecutive batches each producing two or more Critical/High findings, or one defect pattern-propagating across four or more prompts within its own batch, means the adaptive rule failed to contain the risk and the ADR must be revisited by a superseding record. **Zero prompt file edited, and none needs to be** — all 430 remain valid as written, because a capability prompt specifies *what* to build and *which* gates apply, never *how often* review runs (verified before adopting the cadence: §30 names the gate set without a cadence, §33's "one recorded checkpoint" is satisfied by the batch close, §34/§35 carry no timing claim, and the only Phase 6 prompt that mentions adversarial review is Prompt 269, already exempt from batching). **One genuine conflict was found and resolved rather than glossed over:** **166** capability prompts across every remaining phase carry an identical §36 clause — "Only the execution index may release `<NEXT>` or another dependency-clean atomic task **after this task is `VERIFIED`**" — which cannot hold literally when a prompt sits at `COMPLETED` until its batch's Tier C closes. Registered and resolved as **`CON-015`** in `docs/ai-agent-build-prompt-package/00-control/04_CONFLICT_REGISTER.md` (version `0.1.1` → `0.1.2`, no existing row altered): **narrowed, not waived** — within one batch a downstream prompt may be released on its upstream being `COMPLETED`; **across a batch boundary §36 is unchanged** and no prompt in batch N+1 begins until every prompt in batch N is `VERIFIED`. The relaxation is exactly co-extensive with the batch, which is the risk envelope `ADR-0021` already accepted and capped at five, further bounded by all-or-nothing batch verification and by the trigger that cuts a batch wherever a dependency edge would separate a producer from its consumer. Resolving it in the conflict register places the override at precedence rank 3, above the task prompt, so it is explicit rather than an implementation artifact silently contradicting a ratified prompt — the exact failure mode `AGENTS.md`'s own "Instruction precedence" exists to prevent. **Docs-only checkpoint — zero schema/service/UI/test/script/dependency file touched**, so the code gate suite is carried forward unchanged from `CG-S11-PRC-007`'s own fresh run rather than re-run for a diff that cannot affect it; the doc-governance gates were run directly. Files: `docs/adr/ADR-0021-batched-review-and-fix-execution-cadence.md`, `docs/standards/BUILD_EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.md`, `docs/standards/RECURRING_DEFECT_TAXONOMY.md` (new); `AGENTS.md` (instance `0.2.0` → `0.3.0`), `docs/adr/README.md`, `docs/runtime/CHANGE_MANIFEST.md` (`CHG-2026-177`), this file, `docs/runtime/HANDOFF.md` (edited). **`CG-S11-PRC-008` (Prompt 257) still requires fresh, separate, explicit operator authorization before it may begin** — this checkpoint changes how a range is executed, not whether one is authorized.)

**Prior checkpoint note:** 2026-08-07 (`CG-S11-PRC-007` — **Sourcing (Prompt 256)** — `VERIFIED`. Operator instruction: "lanjut prompt 256 sd 260" (continue prompts 256 through 260) — a named-range authorization — immediately narrowed by a follow-up explicit instruction, "hentikan sampe prompt 256" ("stop at/through Prompt 256"), received mid-session before any work began on Prompt 257. Per this build's own standing discipline for narrowing/withdrawing an authorized range mid-flight (no exact prior precedent for a narrowing, but directly analogous to every prior range-boundary precedent's own "closing a range does not itself authorize the next one" rule, applied here in reverse — an explicit narrowing is honored immediately, not overridden by the earlier, now-superseded broader instruction), **only `CG-S11-PRC-007` (Prompt 256) was executed this checkpoint. `CG-S11-PRC-008` through `CG-S11-PRC-011` (Prompts 257–260) were never started** — no file under any of their own eventual scopes was touched, confirmed by `git status` showing only Sourcing-scoped paths. Sixth Phase 6 implementation checkpoint, continuing directly from the already-`VERIFIED` `CG-S11-PRC-006` (Prompt 255). Implements source-linked sourcing requests, vendor discovery, and human-owned candidate-eligibility/shortlist curation — a source-versioned shortlist handoff surface a later, separate Procurement RFQ capability (Prompt 257, **not built this checkpoint**) can read from. Extends, never duplicates, three already-canonical roots: `app.costing_requests` (COM-148), `app.shipment_orders` (OPS-169), and `app.vendor_profiles`/`app.vendor_services`/`app.vendor_coverage` (PRC-251) plus `app.get_vendor_compliance_eligibility` (PRC-253) — all read-only from this migration's perspective. Per `ADR-0020`, canonical vendor identity remains `app.master_records` (`master_type_code='vendor'`) reached via `app.vendor_profiles.master_record_id`; no second vendor-shaped table introduced. New: `app.sourcing_requests` (nine-state lifecycle: `draft→open→shortlisted|closed_no_source`, `draft|open→cancelled`, `shortlisted|closed_no_source|cancelled→open`), `app.sourcing_request_events` (append-only lifecycle history, mirrors `app.vendor_profile_lifecycle_events`), `app.sourcing_candidates` (trigger-enforced vendor identity, structurally constrained four-value `exclusion_reasons`), and two masked/plain `_directory` read views — 3 tables, 15 RPCs. Eligibility evaluation composes real canonical data (vendor lifecycle status, `app.vendor_services`/`app.vendor_coverage`, `app.get_vendor_compliance_eligibility`) with explainable per-candidate exclusion reasons; capacity/availability (`PRC-262`, not yet built) and vendor cargo-type restriction (no master exists anywhere in this repository) are both explicitly disclosed as deferred, never fabricated against data that doesn't exist. Built via background `Workflow` (implement → 3-lens adversarial review — spec-compliance, security/RLS, correctness/concurrency — → fix), independently re-verified by the orchestrating session rather than accepted on the fix agent's own self-report. **The implement agent's own live `db:test` iteration caught and fixed 2 real defects before review even began**: a `text[] || 'literal'` array-append operator-resolution bug (PostgreSQL resolved it against the array-literal overload, not element-append, raising `malformed array literal` on every exclusion path) fixed with `array_append`; and — the more serious of the two — three read RPCs (`get_sourcing_request`/`list_sourcing_requests`/`list_sourcing_candidates`) selected from the masked `_directory` views AFTER their own explicit `evaluate_permission(p_actor_auth_user_id, ...)` check, but those views' own row filter/cost mask default to session-implicit `auth.uid()`, not the RPC's own validated actor parameter — live-reproduced returning all-null/zero rows for a legitimate, explicitly-validated actor outside a real browser JWT session (e.g. any service-role/job-context caller) — fixed by reconstructing the identical masked projection directly against the base table with the actor parameter passed through explicitly, the same technique PRC-255's own `search_vendor_rates` already established for exactly this reason. **Adversarial review (3 parallel lenses — spec-compliance; security/RLS, live-tested against a real disposable Postgres 16 instance with forged/alternate-actor sessions; correctness/concurrency, live-tested with real concurrent `psql` sessions) found and the fix pass closed 6 further real defects, including 1 CRITICAL and 1 HIGH/MEDIUM (duplicate-counted across two reviewers, merged into one fix)**: **[CRITICAL/HIGH]** `demand_snapshot jsonb` carried the full, unmasked source row with zero field-level protection — live-reproduced two independent leaks through the same column: a `proactive` request's own snapshot embedded `budget_amount` verbatim, bypassing the typed column's own `PRC:View cost` mask entirely (read as `demand_snapshot->>'budget_amount'` by a `PRC:View`-only actor who saw `cost_masked=true` on the typed column); and an `operational_demand` request's own snapshot (`to_jsonb(shipment_orders)`) carried real `consignee_snapshot`/`notify_party_snapshot` customer PII — governed on the source row by record-scoped access Sourcing's own tenant+`PRC:View`-only RLS never replicates — traced all the way to the browser's RSC/flight payload via the detail page's server-to-client component prop-passing. Fixed by excluding the two PII keys at write time and masking `budget_amount` at read time in the view and both read RPCs, using the identical `has_prc_view_cost` gate the typed column already used. **[HIGH/MEDIUM, live-reproduced with two concurrent `psql` sessions]** `app.shortlist_sourcing_candidate` read the parent `sourcing_requests.status` via a plain, unlocked `SELECT`, both racing a concurrent `submit_sourcing_shortlist`/`cancel_sourcing_request` (letting an excluded vendor be shortlisted into an already-"frozen" roster) and disclosing the parent's real status/the candidate's own eligibility bit to an unauthorized, zero-permission, fully cross-tenant caller BEFORE the permission check ran — fixed by reordering permission-evaluation ahead of the parent-row read and locking the parent row `for update`, with a deliberate lock-ordering discipline (candidate row before parent row) so this fix and the next cannot deadlock against each other. **[MEDIUM, live-reproduced]** `app.evaluate_sourcing_candidate_eligibility` had the identical unlocked-parent-read gap around its own up-to-500-vendor scan loop — fixed with a locked re-check after the loop completes (not before, to preserve the same non-deadlocking lock order). **[MEDIUM]** no non-negativity `CHECK` existed for cargo weight/volume columns, live-reproduced accepting a negative value via both direct creation and the override RPC's own "null-to-value always widens" carve-out — fixed with four new table `CHECK` constraints. Two further Low findings fixed (missing `mode`/date indexes named in the source spec; a missing REST/GraphQL-parity disclosure sentence in the build log). **Two findings confirmed real but disclosed rather than fixed, each registered as a new tracked issue**: `ISS-2026-041` (Medium — the source spec's own §26 names "Sales/Operations submit/view own source status" but every sourcing RPC gates PRC-only, with no established ownership-scoped cross-module read pattern in this repository to safely copy under review-pressure time constraints) and (registered by the orchestrating session's own independent verification, not the fix agent) `ISS-2026-042` (High per the audit gate's own severity classification, repository-wide — a `js-yaml@4.3.0` transitive devDependency advisory, `CVE-2026-59870`, newly published since Prompt 255 last ran `security:audit` clean; confirmed `package.json`/`pnpm-lock.yaml` genuinely untouched by this checkpoint, a lockfile bump is outside Sourcing's own allowed-files scope). This checkpoint's own fix agent produced a complete, well-reasoned final disposition report and ran `next build` as its literal last verification step — **independently re-verified by the orchestrating session with every gate number matching exactly, including a fresh, from-scratch re-run of the full 139-file `db:test` suite and direct code reads of all three critical/high fixes (the `demand_snapshot` masking at both write- and read-time, and the reordered-permission-then-locked-parent-row shape in `shortlist_sourcing_candidate`) confirming each is genuinely present and correctly shaped**: `typecheck` clean; `pnpm run lint` 0 errors/125 pre-existing warnings (none introduced); `pnpm run test` **3313/3314** (the one failure, `check-worktree-collision.test.ts`, is a pre-existing, environment-git-state-dependent test unrelated to this checkpoint, independently reproduced); `pnpm run db:test` **ALL PASSED**, full 139-file suite including the new `procurement-sourcing.sql` and zero regression to every pre-existing PRC-251..255/COM/OPS/FIN/PLT/ATW suite; `pnpm exec next build` **PASS**, both new sourcing routes present as dynamic routes; `docs:check`/`security:check`/`data-classification:check`/`threat-model:check`/`standards:check`/`git:check-paths` all **PASS**; `security:audit` **FAIL**, confirmed pre-existing and unrelated (see `ISS-2026-042` above, newly registered by this checkpoint's own independent verification pass, not previously tracked anywhere). **`CG-S11-PRC-008` (Prompt 257, Procurement RFQ) is dependency-clean but this checkpoint's own "lanjut prompt 256 sd 260" authorization was explicitly narrowed to Prompt 256 alone before any Prompt 257 work began — fresh, separate, explicit operator authorization is required before Prompt 257 or any further Phase 6 capability may begin.** Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-06/PRC-256.md`, `docs/build-log/phase-06/PROCUREMENT_VENDOR_EXECUTION_INDEX.md`.)

**Prior checkpoint note:** 2026-08-05 (`CG-S10-ATW-032` — **Audit Register Verification and Repair** — `COMPLETED`. Operator instruction: verify and repair, then report whether Prompt 249 is ready. **It is.** This checkpoint is the verification phase `ISS-2026-034` was opened to demand. `ATW-031` ended with 60 unverified candidate findings and one instruction attached: do not fix from the register directly. Every claim was re-derived from the live post-migration catalogue and judged against the ratified design before being treated as a defect. **Disposition of the 32 claims verified this round: 20 CONFIRMED and repaired, 7 ALREADY-FIXED by a later migration, 5 BY-DESIGN with the ratified text quoted.** The register's own warning was correct — the false-positive rate is high, and the two dominant causes are a claim describing code a later migration already changed, and a claim assuming a design constraint the design does not impose. **The two most consequential findings were against this audit's own predecessors.** An idempotency guard that raises `unique_violation` inside a block whose own handler traps `unique_violation` is inert — all 55 guards from `ATW-030`/`ATW-031` were swept structurally (block nesting parsed, not grepped, because a `raise exception` line contains the word 'exception' and a naive scan mistakes it for a block's exception section, which is what hid it); `ingest_milestone_event`'s was genuinely dead, so a `DELIVERED` submission reusing an earlier key returned that event with no error and the delivery was silently discarded. And **74 functions** carried the version predicate on their UPDATE but never checked it applied: execution fell through with an unset composite, `capture_audit_event` wrote a fabricated `success` row with a NULL `tenant_id`, the caller got an all-NULL composite the TypeScript guard does not catch, and a transition-history row already written in the same transaction committed describing a change that never took effect. **Closed:** `ISS-2026-032` (the classification pass it asked for found **33** actionable functions, not the ~315 it estimated — each already validated authority but asked 'is the CLAIMED actor allowed?' rather than 'is the caller that actor?') and `ISS-2026-034`. **`ISS-2026-010`'s premise corrected**: it assumed most tenant-scoped policies already fail closed for a `customer_user`; the completed audit shows **97** SELECT policies test tenant membership and nothing else, and a freshly granted `customer_user` provably satisfies it. The repair is deliberately not attempted — deciding which of the 97 the portal legitimately needs IS the Phase 8 contract. **Gates:** typecheck 0 errors; lint 0 errors / 85 pre-existing warnings; `pnpm test` **3091/3091**; `pnpm run db:test` **ALL PASSED across 155 migrations**; all 8 runnable governance gates pass (`preflight` fails only for a missing `.env` in this container — an environment condition, not code). **`PHASE_5_VERIFIED` remains set. Prompt 249 is ready to run.** `ISS-2026-033`, which `ATW-031` said should block Prompt 250, was closed at `CG-S10-ATW-032`'s own predecessor work in `20260730460000`. Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-05/ATW-032.md`.)

**Prior checkpoint note:** 2026-08-05 (`CG-S10-ATW-031` — **Repository-Wide Defect Closure** — `COMPLETED`. Operator instruction: close every issue, error and defect so Prompt 249 can start from a clean codebase. **The codebase is NOT yet clean, and this checkpoint does not claim it is.** Ten catalogued issues were closed with live evidence and committed regression tests; in the process a 12-way adversarial audit surfaced **60 further candidate findings** and a structural sweep confirmed **a previously-uncatalogued class of cross-tenant authorization holes**. Saying so is the deliverable — declaring the repository clean after ten closures would repeat exactly the error `ATW-029` made when it asserted zero Critical/High open while three High defects were live. **Closed:** `ISS-2026-029` (27 functions / 29 idempotency short-circuits — the sharpest case: `apply_finance_ar_allocation` and `reverse_finance_ar_allocation` write the SAME table under the SAME unique key, so reusing a key to REVERSE an allocation silently did nothing and returned success, on a Finance record; the AP pair and both journal-correction functions share that shape), `ISS-2026-020` (FIFO/FEFO ordering had no unique key — re-scoped from "test flake" to a real policy defect, since it decides which physical lot and which expiry date ships), `ISS-2026-030`, `ISS-2026-012` (the job_type list had drifted 12/10/8/8, leaving two types permanently un-dispatchable), `ISS-2026-028` (fixed at the grant boundary in the database, not the UI — the bypass was reachable by any `OPS:Edit` holder), `ISS-2026-025`, `ISS-2026-017` (enforced at the one authority gate 416 functions share), `ISS-2026-027`, `ISS-2026-007`, `ISS-2026-005` (`ACCEPTED_RISK`). **`ISS-2026-007` was hiding live vulnerabilities:** `pnpm audit` had been broken since `PH0-094` and nobody re-tested it. It works — and found **20 real advisories, 11 high**, four of them in Next.js itself (App Router middleware/proxy bypass, SSRF in Server Actions, SSRF in rewrites, Server Actions DoS), all fixed in `16.2.11` while this repository pinned `16.2.10`. Patched; a new CI gate now fails on critical/high **and** fails when the advisory service is unreachable. **Newly opened — `ISS-2026-033` is the highest-value item in the register:** 91 `SECURITY DEFINER` functions are granted to `authenticated` with **no authority check of any kind**, 30 of them writes. Four spot-verified against the live schema: `app.recalculate_quotation_totals` (rewrites any tenant's quotation money columns), `app.generate_route_planning_candidates` (rewrites another tenant's route plans), `app.evaluate_dispatch_readiness`, `app.next_quotation_number`. Cross-tenant read AND write by any authenticated principal. Not all 91 are defects — several authenticate by token/signature or delegate to a checked callee — so it needs per-function triage across five already-`VERIFIED` phases, which is its own prompt, not an audit checkpoint's call. Also opened: `ISS-2026-034` (60 unverified candidates preserved in `ATW-031-DEEP-AUDIT-FINDINGS.md` — the fan-out's verification phase never ran, so they must be re-derived before anyone acts on them), `ISS-2026-031`, `ISS-2026-032`. **Gates:** typecheck 0 errors; lint 0 errors / 85 pre-existing warnings; `pnpm test` **3085/3085**; `services/gps-gateway` **47/47**; `next build` exit 0, 90 routes; `pnpm run db:test` **ALL PASSED from scratch across 145 migrations**; new `security:audit` reports zero advisories at every severity; all governance gates pass. **`PHASE_5_VERIFIED` remains set.** **Prompt 249 (Phase 6 README) is not blocked. Prompt 250 should be** — `ISS-2026-033` is cross-tenant read/write reachable by any authenticated principal, and Phase 6 will add more surface in the same style to the same schema. **Prior checkpoint note (2026-08-05, `CG-S10-ATW-030` — **Post-Closure Codebase Audit and Repair** — `COMPLETED`. An out-of-band, operator-requested audit of the entire codebase as implemented up to and including Prompt 248, with repairs applied **before** Prompt 249. Not a prompt-package task. **`PHASE_5_VERIFIED` remains set and is not withdrawn.** The full gate suite was re-run fresh at baseline `e381d89` before any edit; `pnpm run db:test` **FAILED** there on `advanced-tms-wms-picking.sql`'s keyrace assertion (`ISS-2026-024`, previously logged as an intermittent flake), which is the thread this checkpoint was pulled from. Every other baseline gate matched `ATW-029`'s reported numbers exactly. **Four defects were found, all open at the moment `PHASE_5_VERIFIED` was declared, each live-reproduced before repair and re-proven by a committed regression test.** **F-1 (High)** — 20 Phase 5 WMS mutation functions matched their idempotent-replay short-circuit on `(tenant_id, idempotency_key)` alone and never checked the found row belonged to the requested target. Reproduced **deterministically, with zero concurrency**: `app.generate_wms_pick_task` called for outbound order line Y under a key already used by line X returned **line X's own task**, with no error. Two damage shapes — silent misattribution, and silent no-op (the requested operation skipped entirely while appearing to succeed: a scanned package line never recorded, a pick never confirmed, a shipment never load-confirmed). `p_idempotency_key` is **user-supplied free text** in this product, so key reuse is an ordinary operator typo, not an attack. Fixed across **29 short-circuits in 20 functions** (`supabase/migrations/20260730380000_...`), applying the already-ratified in-repo pattern from `ATW-020`/`021`/`022`. **`ISS-2026-024` is reclassified from "flaky test" to this real defect** — no content-mismatch check ever existed, and the 2-in-11 failure rate measured the scheduler, not the product being mostly correct. **F-2 (High)** — the GPS Gateway silently dropped any packet arriving while the previous packet's ingest was in flight: no ACK, not buffered, `packetsRejected` stayed `0`. **F-3 (High)** — the durable telemetry buffer destroyed batches enqueued during a flush, losing precisely the telemetry it exists to protect. **F-4 (Low)** — `select("*")` against a column-restricted table (closes `ISS-2026-026`). **Two new issues raised and deliberately not repaired**, each larger than this checkpoint's bounded scope: `ISS-2026-029` (Medium — the same replay defect class still open in ~35 Finance/Operations/Platform-Core/TMS functions across four already-`VERIFIED` phases; Finance is the highest-consequence remaining target) and `ISS-2026-030` (Low-Medium — a torn final line permanently wedges the gateway buffer). Re-verified as **not** currently exploitable: `ISS-2026-017` and the 18 RLS-disabled tenant tables (zero `anon`/`authenticated` grant on every one). **Post-repair gates:** `typecheck` 0 errors; `lint` 0 errors / 85 pre-existing warnings; root `pnpm test` **3084/3084**; `services/gps-gateway` **44/44** (41 + 3 new); `next build` exit 0, 90 routes unchanged; `pnpm run db:test` **ALL PASSED, twice from scratch** — **138 migrations**, all test files green both runs, the previously-intermittent keyrace assertion now deterministic with its assertion never weakened; all 6 governance gates pass. **Correction recorded rather than concealed:** `ADVANCED_TMS_WMS_CLOSURE_REPORT.md` §7's "Zero Critical/High-severity repository-controlled issue is open anywhere" was not accurate — F-1/F-2/F-3 were open. The closure state stands (every defect was repository-controlled, bounded, now closed with live evidence; no Phase 5 capability is missing or unproven), but the claim is corrected in the runtime records rather than by editing that report's committed text. Root cause for future closure checkpoints: `ATW-029` verified that prior evidence existed and re-ran the existing suite, but did not adversarially probe the code — and a re-run of an existing suite cannot surface a defect whose assumptions that suite shares with the code. Next eligible action is unchanged: **Prompt 250**, still requiring fresh explicit operator authorization. Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-05/ATW-030.md`.)**

**Prior checkpoint note (2026-08-05, `ATW-027` — Advanced TMS/WMS Integrity and Security Hardening — `VERIFIED`. Eighth task within the "lanjut prompt 239-248" range, continued via the "lanjut sd 248" instruction. **Two parallel adversarial-probe `Agent` calls, then two parallel fix `Agent` calls, no `Workflow` attempted this checkpoint.** The probes live-reproduced **12 real vulnerabilities across all four GPS source classes named in Prompt 246 §16 (1 Critical, 5 High, 4 Medium, 2 Low) — the most severe finding set of this entire build.** Most seriously, **CRITICAL**: a table-wide `grant select` on `app.third_party_provider_connections` let any authenticated tenant member read `webhook_secret_value` directly — live-reproduced end to end (leak the secret, compute a valid HMAC-SHA256 signature, inject accepted forged telemetry that became the vehicle's live position) — fixed with an explicit 14-column grant excluding the secret, **independently re-verified by this checkpoint's own orchestrating session via direct `information_schema.column_privileges` inspection and a live raw-SQL probe as a low-privilege tenant member (not accepted on the fix agent's own self-report alone)**. Also **HIGH** ×4: an unbounded caller-controlled `event_at` could permanently poison `app.arbitrate_and_project_vehicle_position`'s own comparison baseline, locking out every future real report forever — fixed with a disclosed, reasoned 24-hour future-timestamp bound (a deliberate deviation from the originally-suggested 5-minute ADR-0011-style bound, since several already-`VERIFIED` fixtures use synthetic offsets up to ~9.3 hours); a cross-tenant IMEI collision could permanently deny service to a victim tenant's real device with zero admin remediation path anywhere in the schema — fixed with a cross-tenant registration rejection (per-IMEI advisory lock closing the TOCTOU window) plus a new, narrowly-scoped `app.deregister_gps_device` RPC; a durable telemetry buffer poison-pill let one permanently-failing device block every other device's telemetry indefinitely — fixed by classifying failures permanent-vs-transient (dead-letter the former, halt-and-retry only the latter); revoked driver mobile-tracking consent was never honored mid-session — fixed with a live re-check on every ingestion call. Four **MEDIUM** and two **LOW** findings also fixed: malformed-but-signed fields crashed two RPCs instead of failing cleanly (both now wrapped, with rate-limit bookkeeping preserved); the third-party rate limiter was keyed on a spoofable header, trivially bypassed — now bound to `connection_id`; the raw GPS Gateway TCP server had no idle timeout or connection cap — both added; `anon` lacked schema `USAGE` despite holding `EXECUTE`, undermining the documented anon-callable claim — granted; gateway log redaction was bypassed by the dominant free-text logging pathway — widened to scan string values including the message itself. **Two findings explicitly disclosed, not fixed, as new registered issues**: `ISS-2026-025` (Medium) — a position-based "salami-slicing" residual on `vehicle_source_health.last_location`, a genuine coverage gap this checkpoint's own orchestrating session discovered while reconciling both fix agents' final reports (each had reasonably assumed the other's scope covered it; neither had); `ISS-2026-026` (Low) — a dead-code `select("*")` gap against the now column-restricted table, zero live callers today. Also closed all three findings `ATW-026` itself registered: `ISS-2026-021` (execution-index/build-log documentation bookkeeping — a real `CG-S10-ATW-011B` row added, Tally table re-refreshed), `ISS-2026-022` (a stale migration comment, corrected via a new purely-additive `COMMENT ON FUNCTION`), `ISS-2026-023` (hardcoded race-test tmp paths, now `pg_backend_pid()`-suffixed — fixing this surfaced a genuine psql limitation, `:variable` interpolation does not reach inside a `do $$ ... $$` body, worked around via a session-level `set_config()`/`current_setting()` GUC). **Incidentally discovered, unrelated to this checkpoint's own scope**: 2 of 11 re-runs while verifying the `ISS-2026-023` fix showed a genuine, pre-existing, non-deterministic flake in `ATW-017`'s own already-`VERIFIED` `advanced-tms-wms-picking.sql` — confirmed unrelated to the fix itself (reproduces identically on the original, unmodified file) — recorded as new `ISS-2026-024` (Medium). Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-05/ATW-027.md` §3–§5. This checkpoint's own orchestrating session independently re-ran the full gate suite twice fresh from a clean shell: root `node:test` 3084/3084 (unchanged), `services/gps-gateway` package `test` 41/41 (new coverage for all 7 fixed findings in that package), `db:test` **ALL PASSED — 137 migrations, 133 test files** (134 + 3 new migrations; 0 new db-test files, existing ones extended; the pre-existing `ATW-016` flake, `ISS-2026-020`, did not trigger on either run), all five governance gates pass. **A real, self-caught documentation-ordering bug from the prior checkpoint was also fixed this checkpoint**: `TASK_LEDGER.md`'s own `ATW-026` row had been inserted chronologically out of order (before, not after, `ATW-025`'s own row) — corrected before this checkpoint's own row was appended. `CG-S10-ATW-028` (Prompt 247, Documentation and Handoff) is now dependency-clean, next in ascending order — proceeding directly through Prompt 248 per the operator's own instruction. Supersedes the `ATW-026` checkpoint note below, retained in history.)

Built via the identical `Workflow` structure as `ATW-013`, with the implementing agent explicitly briefed on `ATW-013`'s own four confirmed bug classes so it could proactively close them from its own first draft -- every claimed result independently re-verified by the orchestrating session before this checkpoint was written. Implemented `ATW-014` (WMS Putaway): `app.wms_putaway_tasks`/`app.wms_putaway_confirmations` plus 9 RPCs, every stock-affecting one composing exclusively against `app.post_inventory_movement` (`ATW-015`, `movement_type='transfer'`). All four `ATW-013` bug classes confirmed proactively closed -- none recurred. Adversarial review found and the fix pass closed one genuine, novel defect (a structurally different race shape than the four pre-briefed classes): the destination-capacity check was an unlocked cross-row aggregate read, letting two different putaway tasks racing the same capacity-limited destination jointly overshoot capacity -- live-reproduced with two real concurrent psql sessions, fixed with a destination-row lock. Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-05/ATW-014.md` §3.3. |

Implemented `ATW-016` (Lot, Batch, Serial and Expiry): `app.item_control_policy_versions`/`app.lot_identities`/`app.serial_identities` plus new RPCs -- a versioned draft->published->archived control-policy lifecycle, real lot/serial identity registration with genuine governed-scope uniqueness (a real Postgres `UNIQUE` index for serials, `ATW-014`'s own newest bug-class lesson proactively applied), generic status-lifecycle RPCs, ledger trace reads, and a real read-only FIFO/FEFO allocation-candidate query. All five known bug classes from `ATW-013`/`ATW-014` confirmed proactively closed -- none recurred. Adversarial review found and the fix pass closed a **critical, live-proven cross-owner data leak** (no read RPC enforced owner-account scoping, letting a customer-portal actor read every other owner's lot/serial/policy data in the same tenant -- fixed with a real owner-scope resolution wired into all eight read paths and the new tables' own RLS policies) plus a `record_version` concurrency defect on policy publish independently live-reproduced by two separate reviewers, and two smaller spec gaps. A genuine, disclosed residual limitation (registration RPCs not yet wired into `ATW-013` receiving capture, matching that checkpoint's own precedent) was logged as a new known issue, `ISS-2026-016`, rather than left undocumented. Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-05/ATW-016.md` §3.3. |

Mirrors `app.wms_inbound_orders`/`ATW-012` near-exactly (simpler draft->confirmed->cancelled lifecycle, no dock scheduling), with owner-account read scoping applied throughout (reusing `ATW-016`'s own `app.actor_can_view_owner_scoped_row`) and two known-bug-class fixes applied proactively beyond what the mirrored `ATW-012` file itself does. Implemented `ATW-017` (WMS Picking) -- the first capability that actually reserves real inventory against real demand (`ATW-016A`'s own confirmed outbound order lines), with Prompt 236 §33's own headline acceptance criterion "concurrent orders cannot double-allocate stock" proven, not merely asserted, via two real independent psql client processes racing to allocate against the SAME outbound order line. Five new tables and 15 new RPCs, every stock-affecting one composing exclusively against `app.reserve_inventory`/`app.release_inventory_reservation`/`app.post_inventory_movement` (`ATW-015`) and `app.list_allocation_candidates` (`ATW-016`). `app.generate_wms_pick_task` locks the target outbound order line before computing how much remains unallocated -- the structural mechanism behind the double-allocation guarantee. **Adversarial review found and the fix pass closed three real defects**: a high-severity missing item-scan check on pick-confirm (a picker could grab the wrong item from a non-item-dedicated bin undetected); a medium-severity fail-closed owner-scoped RLS defect (a nested subquery against `app.warehouses` invisible to `customer_user` actors, denying legitimate owners their own data); and a medium-severity idempotency-race gap in composed inserts one level below the outermost create-once check. **This checkpoint's own orchestrating session independently found and fixed a fourth defect** -- the identical RLS fail-closed pattern in `ATW-016A`'s own already-committed policies, widened via `DROP POLICY`/`CREATE POLICY` without editing the applied file -- and corrected a route-count self-report error in the fix agent's own final report (claimed 91; independently re-verified as 90, unchanged). Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-05/ATW-017.md` §3.3-§3.5. |
| `ATW-018` checkpoint (2026-08-04, branch `claude/prompt-231-238-e2wb54`) | Built via the identical background `Workflow` implement/three-lens-review/fix structure used since `ATW-013`, completing cleanly on its first attempt. Implemented `ATW-018` (WMS Packing) -- scan-verified packing grouping `ATW-017`'s own confirmed picks into a traceable package/container hierarchy: five new tables and 18 new RPCs, measured weight/dimensions, QC, seal, outbound readiness, no inventory-ledger interaction at all (disclosed bound). `app.add_wms_package_line` locks the target pick task before computing how much has already been packed -- the double-pack-prevention mechanism, proven with a real two-process concurrent psql race. **Adversarial review found and the fix pass closed a CRITICAL concurrency defect, independently confirmed by two separate review lenses**: `app.reparent_wms_package`'s cycle-prevention ancestor walk locked only its two endpoints, letting two disjoint concurrent reparent calls each pass their own cycle check and together commit a real, permanently-persisted cycle into the package hierarchy -- both reviewers independently live-reproduced a real 4-node cycle against a live database. Fixed by locking every node visited during the walk; Postgres's own deadlock detector now correctly aborts one of any two conflicting concurrent reparents. This checkpoint's own orchestrating session independently corrected a third self-report route-count discrepancy this session (94 vs. 89 claimed; independently re-verified as 90, unchanged) and observed one transient, unrelated timing flake in `ATW-017`'s own concurrency test (already covered by `ISS-2026-014`). Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-05/ATW-018.md` §3.3-§5. |
| `ATW-019` checkpoint (2026-08-04, branch `claude/prompt-231-238-e2wb54`, this checkpoint) | **The eighth and FINAL checkpoint in the operator's own explicit 231-238 range.** Its own first `Workflow` attempt failed outright on a genuine account-level usage-limit wall ("session limit -- resets 3:10am UTC"); a retry after that window completed cleanly. Implemented `ATW-019` (WMS Outbound, narrowed scope) -- scoped to the remainder of Prompt 238 §13 not already built by `ATW-016A`: staging/dock/load/custody, ship lifecycle, idempotent ship-confirm inventory movements, billing-event eligibility. `app.wms_outbound_shipments` is a new ship-execution table referencing `ATW-016A`'s own outbound orders. **The implementer disclosed a genuine correction to its own task brief**: `app.consume_inventory_reservation` (`ATW-015`) is deliberately never called (a fully-resolved pick task's own reservation is `released`, not `active`) -- instead one batched, atomic `post_inventory_movement` (consumption) posts the real stock issue, traceable via a new issue-lines table. `app.wms_billing_eligibility_events` is a genuine new table, never writing to any Finance schema. **Adversarial review found and the fix pass closed two real defects**: a high-severity missing `OPS:Override` gate on partial/backorder ship-confirm (proven by the implementer's own db-test fixture, where a plain staff actor unilaterally approved a partial shipment); and a low-severity, fail-safe cross-shipment aggregate race, fixed with a per-order advisory lock. This checkpoint's own orchestrating session independently corrected a fourth self-report route-count discrepancy this session (89 claimed; independently re-verified as 90, unchanged). Full detail: `docs/build-log/phase-05/ATW-019.md` §3.3-§5. **`ISS-2026-011` is now fully `RESOLVED`. Prompts 231-238 are all `VERIFIED`** -- the operator's own named range is complete. |

Checkpoint summary: Step 2 discovery is genuinely closed and trustworthy (`RUNTIME_DISCOVERY_VERIFIED`, single lineage, no divergence). Step 3 (Prompts 36–48, `docs/architecture/01_*.md`–`13_*.md`) is also genuinely closed and trustworthy — the divergence only affects Prompts 49–51 (`14_*.md`–`16_*.md`) and Phase 0 Prompts 80–82. Two independent agent sessions ran those six task IDs in parallel from the same shared ancestor, producing materially different content (e.g. 607 vs. 401 traced requirement items). This was correctly detected and halted by a prior session (`ERR-2026-002`, `HANDOFF.md` `HO-20260715-021`), which asked an operator to choose one of three reconciliation options before any further work continued. Before that decision was recorded, both branches' pull requests (PR #10, then PR #11) were merged into `main` directly. Because the two lineages' edits did not overlap line-for-line, git resolved both merges without conflict markers by **silently concatenating** the divergent content — not reconciling it. This session (this checkpoint) discovered and documented that outcome as `ERR-2026-003`, consolidated the previously-stacked `docs/runtime/*.md` ledgers into single coherent documents, and halted rather than build further Phase 0 capability prompts on top of an unreliable Step 3/Phase 0 baseline. No product/business decision was reopened — this is a process/governance issue about which of two already-produced documents is authoritative, plus a mechanical cleanup of two duplicated documents.

## 8. Release-readiness summary

Unchanged in substance from every prior checkpoint — Phase 0 closure is a **foundation** milestone, not a release milestone. No business-domain module exists yet.

| Readiness domain | Status |
|---|---|
| All ten module suites | `NOT_STARTED` — Phase 0 introduced zero domain code by design |
| Requirement traceability | Discovery- and Step-3-level evidence complete and trustworthy (`RUNTIME_DISCOVERY_VERIFIED`, `RUNTIME_ARCHITECTURE_VERIFIED`); Phase-0-level traceability (`PHASE_0_VERIFIED`) also now complete. Phase 1+ requirement traceability is `NOT_STARTED`. |
| Tenant/security · Finance/data · E2E/regression · Migration/backup/DR · Performance/accessibility · Observability/docs | `NOT_STARTED` at the product level — foundation-level contracts and tooling for these are real and tested (§5), but no domain surface exists yet for them to protect |
| Go/no-go approval | `NOT_STARTED` |

External pilot is not a release stage. Direct GA requires the entire table `VERIFIED` with zero open Sev-1/critical defects. **Phase 0 closing does not change this — it only unblocks Phase 1 to begin building toward it.**

## 10. Update rules

Update after every atomic task, rollback, gate change, blocker change, or checkpoint. Reconcile with `TASK_LEDGER.md`, build logs, change manifest, error/issue ledgers. Status is controlled by the evidence link. Keep this file as **one** current-state dashboard — if a future merge produces stacked/duplicate sections again, consolidate them in the same checkpoint that discovers them rather than leaving them stacked.
